      title: "Anticipating the Author’s Purpose",
      body: "\n    <p>The first and perhaps most important thing to remember about approaching an RC passage is a core skill we have already learned when practicing Logical Reasoning Questions: We must be aware of the passage structurally.</p>\n    <p>Most LR stimuli consisted of arguments. In an argument, there will be a main conclusion and supporting premises. When approaching an LR question argument, we consistently ask ourselves, “what is the author’s main point?” or “what is the main conclusion of this argument?” This applied not only to Find the Conclusion Questions but to every question type from the Assumption Family (Flaw, SA, NA, Strengthen, Weaken, etc.) and some more. When reading an RC passage, that is precisely the same approach we should take. As we go through the passage, we are constantly asking ourselves, <strong>“what is the main point of this passage? What is the author trying to convey, and what kind of supporting information are they using?”</strong></p>\n    <p>But then again, RC passages are much longer than the arguments we see in LR stimuli. As a result, many of us end up mindlessly reading/browsing through the information without really thinking about what the author is trying to say. We end up finishing the passage but can’t remember anything. The bits and pieces of information we recall are fragmented and piecemeal. When we finally get to the questions, they stress us out, and we have no idea what to do.</p>\n    <p>Because time is so limited in RC, we don’t have the luxury of wasting any of it. We must engage with the passage from the beginning and practice <strong>active reading</strong>. Rather than mindlessly wandering through the passage, we need to ask ourselves questions, find the answers in the passages, and reorganize the information we just read structurally.</p>\n    <p>Let me show you how:</p>",
    }
    ],

In other words, as soon as we begin reading a passage, we ask ourselves, “why is the author writing this passage?” Just like how each LR argument will contain a main conclusion, and all that information will make sense once you have isolated what the conclusion is; if we can figure out the main point/conclusion of a passage, then reading and understanding the passage will become so much easier.</p>",

Its whole 17-test suite never ran. A previous investigation cleared\n * the file and was right to: it was byte-identical to origin/main, valid UTF-8,\n * and `node --check` passed on both of its imports. The CRLF was on the\n * imported `scripts/knowledge/*.mjs`, and the reported position was not where\n * the problem was.\n *\n * Proved 2026-09-09 by converting only those two `.mjs` files to CRLF in an\n * otherwise clean checkout: the suite failed identically, and converting them\n * back made it pass.\n *\n * `.gitattributes` keeps checkouts LF. This is the part that speaks up when a\n * tool rewrites a file anyway — git normalises on add under\n * `core.autocrlf=input`, so a CRLF working copy is still \"clean\" and nothing\n * else in the repo would notice.\n *\n * Reads the files rather than importing them, so it still runs when the thing\n * it is checking is exactly what is broken.\n */\n\nconst ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '../../..');\n\n/** Directories with no bearing on what vitest or the bundler parses. */\nconst SKIP_DIRS = new Set(['node_modules', 'dist', '.git', 'coverage', 'playwright-report']);\n\nconst EXTENSIONS = new Set(['.ts', '.tsx', '.mjs', '.cjs', '.js', '.jsx']);\n\nfunction walk(dir: string, out: string[] = []): string[] {\n  let entries: fs.Dirent[];\n  try {\n    entries = fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true });\n  } catch {\n    return out;\n  }\n  for (const entry of entries) {\n    if (entry.name.startsWith('.') || SKIP_DIRS.has(entry.name)) continue;\n    const full = path.join(dir, entry.name);\n    if (entry.isDirectory()) walk(full, out);\n    else if (EXTENSIONS.has(path.extname(entry.name))) out.push(full);\n  }\n  return out;\n}\n\nfunction crlfFiles(dir: string) {\n  return walk(path.join(ROOT, dir))\n    .filter((file) => fs.readFileSync(file).includes('\\r\\n'))\n    .map((file) => path.relative(ROOT, file).replace(/\\\\/g, '/'));\n}\n\ndescribe('source files use LF', () => {\n  it('has no CRLF under scripts/', () => {\n    // The one that actually bit: tests import these through Vite.\n    expect(crlfFiles('scripts')).toEqual([]);\n  });\n\n  it('has no CRLF under src/', () => {\n    expect(crlfFiles('src')).toEqual([]);\n  });\n});\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu-wt-notes\\src\\lib\\__tests__\\lineEndings.test.ts", "content": "import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';\nimport fs from 'node:fs';\nimport path from 'node:path';\n\n/**\n * A shebang plus CRLF makes a script unimportable, and nothing says so.\n *\n * `questionStatsBuilder.test.ts` sat broken on main with\n * `SyntaxError: Invalid or unexpected token` pointed at its own line 4 — a\n * comment. Its whole 17-test suite never ran. A previous investigation cleared\n * the test file and was right to: it was byte-identical to origin/main, valid\n * UTF-8, and `node --check` passed on both of its imports. The problem was in\n * `scripts/knowledge/build-question-stats.mjs`, which the reported position\n * never pointed at.\n *\n * Isolated 2026-09-09 in a clean checkout, one variable at a time. CRLF on the\n * test file alone passes. CRLF on that one import fails. The same file with the\n * same CRLF and its `#!/usr/bin/env node` removed passes. Both halves are\n * needed, which is why the 1,796 CRLF files under src/ are fine — nothing\n * strips a shebang from them.\n *\n * `.gitattributes` keeps `scripts/**` LF on checkout. This is the part that\n * speaks up when a tool rewrites a file anyway: git normalises on add under\n * `core.autocrlf=input`, so a CRLF working copy still reads as clean and\n * nothing else in the repo would notice.\n *\n * Reads the files rather than importing them, so it still runs when the thing\n * it is checking is exactly what is broken.\n */\n\nconst SCRIPTS = path.resolve(__dirname, '../../../scripts');\n\nfunction walk(dir: string, out: string[] = []): string[] {\n  let entries: fs.Dirent[];\n  try {\n    entries = fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true });\n  } catch {\n    return out;\n  }\n  for (const entry of entries) {\n    if (entry.name.startsWith('.') || entry.name === 'node_modules') continue;\n    const full = path.join(dir, entry.name);\n    if (entry.isDirectory()) walk(full, out);\n    else if (/\\.(mjs|cjs|js)$/.test(entry.name)) out.push(full);\n  }\n  return out;\n}\n\ndescribe('a script a test can import', () => {\n  it('never combines a shebang with CRLF line endings', () => {\n    const broken = walk(SCRIPTS)\n      .filter((file) => {\n        const source = fs.readFileSync(file);\n        return source.subarray(0, 2).toString() === '#!' && source.includes('\\r\\n');\n      })\n      .map((file) => path.relative(path.dirname(SCRIPTS), file).replace(/\\\\/g, '/'));\n\n    // If this fails, convert the listed files to LF. Nothing about their\n    // contents is wrong.\n    expect(broken).toEqual([]);\n  });\n\n  it('still has the two that questionStatsBuilder imports', () => {\n    // A rename would make the check above vacuously true.\n    for (const name of ['build-question-stats.mjs', 'parse-lr-explanations.mjs']) {\n      expect(fs.existsSync(path.join(SCRIPTS, 'knowledge', name))).toBe(true);\n    }\n  });\n});\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\.claude\\projects\\C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os\\memory\\tailwind-prose-classes-are-inert.md", "content": "---\nname: tailwind-prose-classes-are-inert\ndescription: LSAT U's prose classes emitted nothing for months; the plugin is now registered with the app's own token palette in both keys.\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\n`@tailwindcss/typography` was in `package.json` but not in `tailwind.config.ts`'s `plugins`\narray. Measured 2026-09-09: zero `.prose` rules in the served stylesheets, and a rendered\n`<h2>` inside `.prose` computed to the inherited body colour at 16px. Thirteen live\nsurfaces were styling markdown with classes that did not exist.\n\nRegistered 2026-09-09 (PR #108). Registering alone would have made things worse: the app\nis dark at `:root` and flips to light with a `.light` class, so `darkMode: [\"class\"]`\nmeans the `dark:` variant almost never fires, and the call sites written as\n`dark:prose-invert` would have landed on Tailwind's gray-700 body text on a near-black\nground.\n\n**Why:** both the `DEFAULT` and `invert` typography keys now resolve from `--foreground`,\n`--muted-foreground`, `--border` and `--muted`. Prose is correct in either theme whether\nor not a call site asks for `prose-invert`.\n\n**How to apply:** never restore Tailwind's own gray palette to either key — that\nreintroduces the dark-mode failure. `.classroom-prose` is separate and stays hand-written.\nTeacher-side markdown outside `.classroom-root` uses `TEACHER_MARKDOWN_CLASS` in\n`src/components/teacher/teacherMarkdown.ts`, which is explicit arbitrary variants.\n\nRelated: [[classroom-prose-loses-list-markers]], [[shebang-plus-crlf-breaks-vitest]]\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\.claude\\projects\\C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os\\memory\\shebang-plus-crlf-breaks-vitest.md", "content": "---\nname: shebang-plus-crlf-breaks-vitest\ndescription: A scripts/*.mjs with a shebang AND CRLF cannot be imported by a vitest test, and the error points at the wrong file.\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\nA `.mjs` under `scripts/` that starts with `#!/usr/bin/env node` **and** has CRLF line\nendings cannot be imported by a vitest test. It fails with `SyntaxError: Invalid or\nunexpected token` reported against **line 4 of the importing test file** — a comment line,\nnowhere near the actual problem.\n\nThis silently skipped all 17 tests in `src/lib/__tests__/questionStatsBuilder.test.ts` on\n`main`. An earlier investigation cleared that file and was correct to: it was\nbyte-identical to origin/main, valid UTF-8, and `node --check` passed on both imports.\n\n**Why:** both halves are required. Isolated 2026-09-09 one variable at a time — CRLF on\nthe test file alone passes; CRLF on `scripts/knowledge/build-question-stats.mjs` alone\nfails; the same CRLF file with its shebang removed passes. That is why 1,796 CRLF files\nunder `src/` are harmless: nothing strips a shebang from them.\n\n**How to apply:** when a vitest suite fails to load with a syntax error at a position that\nmakes no sense, check the line endings of what it imports, not the file named. `git\nstatus` will not help — `core.autocrlf=input` normalises on add, so a CRLF working copy\nreads as clean. `.gitattributes` pins `scripts/**` to LF and\n`src/lib/__tests__/lineEndings.test.ts` fails loudly if one appears anyway.\n\nRelated: [[classroom-prose-loses-list-markers]]\n"}

Thirteen live surfaces were styling markdown with classes that did\nnot exist.\n\nRegistering it alone would have made several of them worse. The app is dark at\n:root and flips to light with a .light class, so darkMode: [\"class\"] means the\ndark: variant almost never fires, and the four call sites written as\ndark:prose-invert would have landed on Tailwind's own gray-700 body text\nagainst a near-black ground.\n\nSo both the DEFAULT and invert keys resolve from --foreground,\n--muted-foreground, --border and --muted. ## assistant

All three done, pushed to [PR #108](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/108) (9 commits now).

## 1. Typography plugin registered

I under-reported the scale before: **13 live surfaces**, not four, were styling markdown with classes that emitted nothing.

Registering it alone would have made several of them *worse*. Your app is dark at `:root` and flips to light with `.light`, so `darkMode: ["class"]` means the `dark:` variant almost never fires — every `dark:prose-invert` call site would have landed on Tailwind's gray-700 text on a near-black ground. So both the `DEFAULT` and `invert` keys resolve from your own `--foreground` / `--muted-foreground` / `--border` tokens. Measured on the production build: **18.73:1 light, 17.95:1 dark**, h2 at 20px/700, disc + decimal markers, links underlined.

## 2. Four orphans deleted

`pages/ClassroomRecaps`, `RecapsSection`, `pages/ClassroomItem`, `teacher/TeacherRecaps`. One detail worth knowing: the dead `pages/ClassroomItem.tsx` had a `whitespace-pre-wrap` on the markdown body that the live view didn't — **the dead copy rendered a recap better than the one students actually used.**

## 3. `questionStatsBuilder.test.ts` — it was never that file

The error pointed at its own line 4, a comment. The previous investigation cleared the file and was **right to** — byte-identical, valid UTF-8, `node --check` passing. The problem was `scripts/knowledge/build-question-stats.mjs`. Isolated one variable at a time:

| | |
|---|---|
| CRLF on the test file alone | passes |
| CRLF on `build-question-stats.mjs` alone | **fails** |
| same file, same CRLF, shebang removed | **passes** |

A **shebang and CRLF together**. That's why 1,796 CRLF files under `src/` are harmless — nothing strips a shebang from them. `.gitattributes` pins `scripts/**` to LF, and `lineEndings.test.ts` guards it by *reading* the files rather than importing them. I normalised the 38 shebanged scripts in your main checkout too, so the suite passes there right now (17 tests, content unchanged, git records no diff).

## What your logged-in session caught

Opening the build in your browser closed the verification gap — and found a real one. The dossier's new **Posted to their Classroom** section showed titles and dates and nothing else, because every posted note is `content_html`, not markdown. Fixed with a plain-text excerpt fallback.

**Two things for you, not bugs in this PR:**

- On Sofia's dossier, two of her four posted notes are marked **hidden from the student** — including what looks like a duplicate of "The Famous Flaws: the most common ones." Worth a look.
- `src/pages/ClassroomTAAssignmentDetail.tsx` is a fifth orphan of the same kind. Left in place — it's the assignment surface, not this one. Say the word.

## Test status

`tsc` exit 0 · `eslint` clean · **4,398 of 4,405 passing**.

The 5 failures are in `BookingOfferHub.test.tsx` and `SingleSession.test.tsx`. They fail in isolation too, and neither imports anything this branch touches — those two files are unchanged from `origin/main`, so that run *is* the origin/main result. Pre-existing, and I did not investigate them.

The static server on `localhost:4399` is still up so you can keep clicking around.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.

Picked up a handoff on the classroom remediation with the instruction to audit and improve the Notes and recaps component overall, for students and for Joshua. The audit found the live reading view printed `content_markdown` as one `<p>`, so every AI-TA session recap reached students as a run-on line with its own markdown syntax showing. Joshua then said yes to all three follow-ups I raised (register `@tailwindcss/typography`, delete the dead files, fix `questionStatsBuilder.test.ts`), then "delete the fifth orphan too and merge it."

## Decisions locked + what shipped

Wired into `ClassroomItemView`. `noteExcerpt` strips markdown for the landing preview. 8 tests.

- `.classroom-prose ul/ol` list markers restored — Tailwind preflight sets `list-style: none` app-wide and the stylesheet only restored the indent. Numbered lists had no numbers. Verified on Joshua's real "Conditional Logic cheat sheet" note that the new bullets read as intended.

- `@tailwindcss/typography` registered — `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\tailwind.config.ts`. Both `DEFAULT` and `invert` keys resolve from `--foreground`/`--muted-foreground`/`--border`/`--muted`. Reason: app is dark at `:root`, light via `.light`, so `dark:prose-invert` never fires; Tailwind's gray palette would have been unreadable on dark. 13 surfaces used prose classes that emitted nothing. Measured on the production build: 18.73:1 light, 17.95:1 dark.
